# SSM seeded patch and document reference catalogs are synthesized rather than AWS's published catalog (5 causes)

> _Note: this issue was identified against the code base at commit `ecac6864d8338f8e9767515c1f51ca4d1624082a`. If you are an issue fixer working on this, the code base you are currently working in can have drifted, so you might need to trace the issue back to the current code base._

## Operation
DescribeAvailablePatches

## Description
5 net-new causes, all in the ssm crate's seeded READ-ONLY reference catalogs — the Windows/Microsoft patch catalog behind DescribeAvailablePatches and DescribeEffectivePatchesForPatchBaseline, and the Amazon-owned SSM document catalog behind ListDocuments. In every case the emulator answers HTTP 200 with a machine-GENERATED catalog (template titles, placeholder/boot-relative timestamps, synthesized ids and URLs) instead of the catalog AWS publishes, so the records a client reads are well-formed but fabricated and none of AWS's real entries resolve. All five share that one fault: the seeded catalog tables are generated placeholders, not AWS's published reference data.

Cause 1 of 5: DescribeAvailablePatches serves a generated patch catalog, so no patch AWS publishes is present and the records that are served carry different ContentUrls (fixture ssm-catalog-describe-available-patches, step task#1).
AWS: Smithy: DescribeAvailablePatches output Patches -> PatchList of Patch{Id, ReleaseDate, Title, Description, ContentUrl, Vendor, ProductFamily, Product, Classification, MsrcSeverity, KbNumber, MsrcNumber, Language, ...}; Patch$Id is documented "The ID of the patch. Applies to Windows patches only. This ID isn't the same as the Microsoft Knowledge Base ID", Patch$ContentUrl "The URL where more information can be obtained about the patch", Patch$KbNumber "The Microsoft Knowledge Base ID of the patch". The operation is a read of AWS's own copy of the vendor update catalogue, so each returned record is a REAL catalogue entry: a stable catalogue GUID in Id, the real KB id in KbNumber, a ContentUrl of the form https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/kb/<kb-digits> matching that KbNumber, Microsoft's own Title and Description text, the vendor's actual ReleaseDate, MsrcSeverity from MSRC and MsrcNumber '' where the patch has no MSRC bulletin.
Emulator: status 200, but the served Patches list shares no entry with AWS's. Roughly seventy of the recording's patch ids resolve to nothing at all — the replay keys $.Patches[Id="88879e50-8a32-4cef-ad64-bfe72d4b2d8f"], $.Patches[Id="b053c9c8-edad-4b8c-a9bb-b33a826c2427"], $.Patches[Id="87caf7bf-f67e-4750-8ec5-578d6baa6a41"], $.Patches[Id="02605070-d2e6-3e57-c41d-80cdf875d3b5"] ... $.Patches[Id="f43db436-7f4b-482b-b177-1ee1adcc8c8d"] are each reported `missing (expected {...}, got None)` — and the twelve records the emulator does place at $.Patches[0] .. $.Patches[11] disagree on ContentUrl, i.e. their ContentUrl is not the support.microsoft.com/en-us/kb/<kb> URL of the KbNumber in the same record.
Why: Patch Manager clients resolve a specific patch by Id or KbNumber to decide approval, to build an InstallOverrideList, or to show the vendor bulletin. Against this emulator every lookup of a real Microsoft patch id or KB misses, and the ContentUrl offered for a patch does not point at that patch's bulletin, so a client either errors on an id AWS always serves or links the user to the wrong vendor page.
Fix: the ssm crate's seeded patch catalogue (state) that DescribeAvailablePatches reads, plus that handler's filter/projection — it must serve AWS's published catalogue entries verbatim (id, KbNumber, ContentUrl derived from the KbNumber, Title, Description, ReleaseDate, MsrcSeverity, MsrcNumber) rather than synthesizing records.
Traces: ssm-catalog-describe-available-patches#task#1.

Cause 2 of 5: the same generated catalogue is served for a second product filter, so the WindowsServer2022 patch set is absent too (fixture ssm-catalog-describe-available-patches, step task#2).
AWS: Smithy: DescribeAvailablePatches input Filters -> PatchOrchestratorFilterList (0..5 filters) selects over the same Patch records as cause 1. The recorded response for this product filter returns the real WindowsServer2022 entries, e.g. Id '2beefd72-9bae-4e75-8e85-70de0fd7bf12' / KbNumber 'KB5006364' / Classification 'Updates' / MsrcSeverity 'Unspecified' / ContentUrl 'https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/kb/5006364' / Title '2021-11 Cumulative Update for .NET Framework 3.5 and 4.8 for Microsoft server operating system version 21H2 for x64 (KB5006364)' and its ARM64 sibling 'a1d52a4f-e584-46ee-8656-156317c0b809', plus KB5009470 entries.
Emulator: status 200 and each of those ids is `missing (... got None)` — thirty-plus keyed ids absent, and $.Patches[0].ContentUrl again mismatched. Filtering therefore selects over synthesized rows, so a product-scoped read returns a catalogue that overlaps AWS's not at all.
Why: a client that scopes DescribeAvailablePatches to one Product (the normal way to review what a baseline will approve for an OS) gets a list in which none of the vendor's actual updates for that OS appear, so patch-approval review and compliance reporting are computed against fabricated rows.
Fix: same component as cause 1 — the seeded patch catalogue must contain the real per-Product entries so the Filters selection returns them.
Traces: ssm-catalog-describe-available-patches#task#2.

Cause 3 of 5: a ServicePacks-classified read of the catalogue returns none of AWS's service-pack entries (fixture ssm-create-patch-baseline-and-inspect-patches, step task#2).
AWS: Smithy: same DescribeAvailablePatches / Patch shape. The recorded response returns the real Windows 7 / Windows Server 2008 R2 SP1 records — Classification 'ServicePacks', KbNumber 'KB976932', ContentUrl 'https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/kb/976932', Titles 'Windows 7 Service Pack 1 (KB976932)', 'Windows 7 Service Pack 1 for x64-based Systems (KB976932)' and 'Windows Server 2008 R2 Service Pack 1 for x64-based Systems (KB976932)', with Microsoft's description text, MsrcSeverity 'Unspecified' and the vendor ReleaseDate.
Emulator: status 200 with $.Patches[0], $.Patches[1] and $.Patches[2] all `missing (expected {...}, got None)` — the first page of the ServicePacks read contains none of the three entries AWS returns.
Why: this is the read a client performs right after creating a patch baseline to inspect what the baseline's rules will match; it sees a service-pack set that does not contain the service packs the OS actually has, so the baseline it then builds approves nothing real.
Fix: same component as cause 1 — the seeded catalogue (state) must carry the ServicePacks-classified entries, and the handler must return them for that classification.
Traces: ssm-create-patch-baseline-and-inspect-patches#task#2.

Cause 4 of 5: DescribeEffectivePatchesForPatchBaseline answers with template-generated patch records bearing boot-relative future release dates instead of the catalogue's real entries (fixture ssm-describe-effective-patches-for-new-baseline, step task#2).
AWS: Smithy: DescribeEffectivePatchesForPatchBaseline output EffectivePatches -> EffectivePatchList of EffectivePatch{Patch, PatchStatus}; Patch as in cause 1, PatchStatus{DeploymentStatus, ComplianceLevel, ApprovalDate}. The recording returns real catalogue entries judged against the baseline — e.g. Patch{Id '11689671-f219-4480-bcd5-d2d4dbd5a6b6', KbNumber 'KB972455', Classification 'ServicePacks', Product 'Windows7', ContentUrl 'https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/kb/972455', Title 'Windows Server Update Services 3.0 SP2 (KB972455)', MsrcSeverity 'Unspecified', MsrcNumber '', Language 'All', Microsoft's description text} with PatchStatus{DeploymentStatus 'APPROVED', ComplianceLevel 'UNSPECIFIED', ApprovalDate} — and a sibling entry 'abd81550-fb19-4311-8af5-74e410e312eb' for the same KB. The Patch members AWS populates for a Windows patch are the Windows-only ones; the Linux-only members (Epoch, Version, Release, Arch, Name, Repository, AdvisoryIds, BugzillaIds, CVEIds) are not part of a Windows record in the recording.
Emulator: status 200, but every EffectivePatches entry is a synthesized row, so $.EffectivePatches[0] through $.EffectivePatches[3] are all reported `missing`/divergent. The served $.EffectivePatches[0].Patch is {Id '36260d86-224a-d1b2-5a04-a9d7cd984f2b', Title '2026-02 ServicePacks for Windows7 (KB5031302)', Description 'ServicePacks published by Microsoft for Windows7 in the 2026-02 release.', KbNumber 'KB5031302', ContentUrl 'https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/kb/5031302', Classification 'ServicePacks', MsrcSeverity 'Important', ReleaseDate 1770746400, Epoch 0} with PatchStatus{DeploymentStatus 'APPROVED', ComplianceLevel 'UNSPECIFIED', ApprovalDate 1771351200}. The Title and Description are a '<YYYY-MM> <Classification> for <Product> (KB<n>)' / '<Classification> published by Microsoft for <Product> in the <YYYY-MM> release.' template, the ReleaseDate/ApprovalDate are generated relative to the emulator's clock (a release date in the future of the recording), and the Windows record carries the Linux-only Epoch member the recorded AWS record does not send. $.EffectivePatches[0].Patch.ContentUrl is keyed as a mismatch for the same reason.
Why: this operation is how a client previews which patches a baseline approves before it is deployed. The preview it gets lists patches that do not exist, with titles and release dates generated from the current date, so approval rules keyed on ReleaseDate/Classification/KbNumber are evaluated against invented data and the preview cannot be reconciled with any real update.
Fix: the ssm crate's seeded patch catalogue (state) and the DescribeEffectivePatchesForPatchBaseline projection — effective patches must be computed by applying the baseline's rules to the real seeded catalogue entries rather than generating Patch records from a title/date template, and a Windows patch record must not emit the Linux-only Patch members.
Traces: ssm-describe-effective-patches-for-new-baseline#task#2.

Cause 5 of 5: ListDocuments projects the Amazon-owned document catalogue with placeholder metadata — a boot/epoch-constant CreatedDate, no Categories, the wrong DocumentFormat, and PlatformTypes/SchemaVersion/TargetType/DocumentType/Author/ReviewStatus values that are not the published ones — and one published document is absent (fixture ssm-catalog-list-documents, step task#1).
AWS: Smithy: ListDocuments output DocumentIdentifiers -> DocumentIdentifierList of DocumentIdentifier{Name, CreatedDate (timestamp), DisplayName, Owner, VersionName, PlatformTypes, DocumentVersion, DocumentType (enum), SchemaVersion, DocumentFormat (enum JSON|YAML), TargetType, Tags, Requires, ReviewStatus, Author}. Each Amazon-owned document has its own publication CreatedDate, its own DocumentFormat (the newer AWS-* automation runbooks are published as YAML, not JSON), a PlatformTypes list restricted to the platforms that document actually supports (a Windows-only runbook lists Windows alone), its published SchemaVersion, its declared TargetType, and — recorded behavior only, Categories is not a member of DocumentIdentifier in the on-disk model — a Categories list such as ['InstanceManagement'], ['ConfigurationManagement'] or ['PatchManagement'].
Emulator: status 200 with per-document metadata that is generated rather than published. CreatedDate is a constant placeholder for most entries — 1451606400 (1970-epoch-plus-2016 constant) for AWS-ASGEnterStandby (expected 1518661792.37), AWS-ASGExitStandby (expected 1518661798.115), AWS-ApplyChefRecipes, AWS-ApplyDSCMofs, AWS-ApplyPatchBaseline and dozens more, and a boot-relative 1758834443.39 for AWS-ApplyAnsiblePlaybooks (expected 1568312907.315). Categories is absent wherever AWS sends one (AWS-ASGEnterStandby, AWS-ASGExitStandby, AWS-ApplyAnsiblePlaybooks, AWS-ApplyChefRecipes, AWS-ApplyPatchBaseline, ... all report `missing (expected ['InstanceManagement'|'ConfigurationManagement'|'PatchManagement'], got None)`). DocumentFormat is reported 'JSON' where AWS publishes 'YAML' (AWS-AddOpsItemDedupStringToEventBridgeRule, AWS-ArchiveEBSVolumeSnapshots, AWS-InstallWindowsUpdates and a long tail of AWS-Enable*/AWS-Diagnose*/AWS-Bulk* runbooks). PlatformTypes carries entries AWS does not list — `PlatformTypes[2]: extra (expected None, got 'MacOS')` on AWS-ApplyChefRecipes, `PlatformTypes[1]: extra (expected None, got 'Linux')` and `[2] 'MacOS'` on AWS-ApplyDSCMofs and AWS-ApplyPatchBaseline, and similar extras on AWS-ConfigureDocker, AWS-ConfigureWindowsUpdate, AWS-InstallMissingWindowsUpdates, AWS-JoinDirectoryServiceDomain. SchemaVersion, TargetType, DocumentType (AWS-InstallAmazonECSAgent, AWS-PasswordReset) and Author/Description/ReviewStatus (AWS-HelloWorldChangeTemplate) likewise diverge, and $.DocumentIdentifiers[Name="AWS-ConfigureS3BucketPublicAccessBlock"] is missing from the listing altogether.
Why: clients discover runbooks by listing and then branch on this metadata — they pick a document for a target OS from PlatformTypes, fetch content as the DocumentFormat the listing advertises, group the console view by Categories, and order or cache by CreatedDate. Against this emulator a Windows-only runbook advertises Linux and macOS support, a YAML runbook is fetched as JSON, Categories is null everywhere, and every document appears to have been created at the same instant, so discovery-driven automation selects the wrong document or parses it with the wrong parser. Open issue 20261119T003828Z-ssm-seeded-built-in-catalogs-are-incomplete-listdocuments.md ("SSM seeded built-in catalogs are incomplete: ListDocuments, ListDocumentVersions and GetInventorySchema") covers the same area and the same fixture step, but the replay reports this cluster as unowned — its divergence keys are not the keys that issue carries (the Tags omission it keys on is gone, while the CreatedDate/DocumentFormat/PlatformTypes/Categories/DocumentType/Author set keyed here is not covered) — so this cause is not gated by it and is filed here.
Fix: the ssm crate's seeded Amazon-owned document catalogue (state) and the ListDocuments response projection: each seeded document must carry its published CreatedDate, DocumentFormat, PlatformTypes, SchemaVersion, DocumentType, TargetType, Author, ReviewStatus and Categories instead of constants defaulted at seed time, and the missing published document must be seeded.
Traces: ssm-catalog-list-documents#task#1.

Id 977e0595-42da-4b87-8444-f692ae55155d, Product WindowsServer2008R2, Title "Windows Server 2008 R2 Service Pack 1 for x64-based Systems (KB976932)", got None)'; '$.Patches[2]: missing (expected ... Cause 5 — ssm-catalog-list-documents#task#1, ListDocuments, 200 vs 200. detail: '$.DocumentIdentifiers[Name="AWS-ASGEnterStandby"].Categories: missing (expected [\'InstanceManagement\'], got None)'; '.CreatedDate: value_mismatch (expected 1518661792.37, got 1451606400)'; same Categories/CreatedDate pair for AWS-ASGExitStandby (expected 1518661798.115); 'AWS-AddOpsItemDedupStringToEventBridgeRule.CreatedDate: value_mismatch (expected 1620236550.997, got 1451606400)' and '.DocumentFormat: value_mismatch (expected \'YAML\', got \'JSON\')'; 'AWS-ApplyAnsiblePlaybooks.CreatedDate: value_mismatch (expected 1568312907.315, got 1758834443.39)'; 'AWS-ApplyChefRecipes.PlatformTypes[2]: extra (expected None, got \'MacOS\')'; 'AWS-ApplyDSCMofs.PlatformTypes[1]: extra (expected None, got \'Linux\')' and '[2]: extra (... \'MacOS\')'. divergence_key additionally keys SchemaVersion (AWS-ApplyPatchBaseline, AWS-ConfigureWindowsUpdate, AWS-FindWindowsUpdates, AWS-RunShellScript, AWS-RunPowerShellScript, AWS-RefreshAssociation), TargetType (AWS-ArchiveEBSVolumeSnapshots, AWS-AttachEBSVolume, AWS-CopySnapshot, AWS-CreateSnapshot, AWS-DeleteSnapshot, ...), DocumentType (AWS-InstallAmazonECSAgent, AWS-PasswordReset), Author/Description/ReviewStatus/PlatformTypes[0..2] (AWS-HelloWorldChangeTemplate), and the wholly absent $.DocumentIdentifiers[Name="AWS-ConfigureS3BucketPublicAccessBlock"].

## Steps to Reproduce
1. git clone the repository and boot the emulator built from that checkout.
2. Replay the wire fixture corpus for trace set simparity-wire-aws-v1 against it.
3. Cause 1: replay fixture ssm-catalog-describe-available-patches step task#1 (DescribeAvailablePatches) and look up the recorded patch ids (e.g. 88879e50-8a32-4cef-ad64-bfe72d4b2d8f / KB5044030) in the served Patches list — they are absent; then compare $.Patches[0..11].ContentUrl against the KbNumber in the same record.
4. Cause 2: replay fixture ssm-catalog-describe-available-patches step task#2 (DescribeAvailablePatches, WindowsServer2022 product filter) and look up 2beefd72-9bae-4e75-8e85-70de0fd7bf12 / KB5006364 — absent.
5. Cause 3: replay fixture ssm-create-patch-baseline-and-inspect-patches step task#2 (DescribeAvailablePatches, ServicePacks) and compare $.Patches[0..2] with the recorded KB976932 Windows 7 / Windows Server 2008 R2 SP1 entries.
6. Cause 4: replay fixture ssm-describe-effective-patches-for-new-baseline step task#2 (DescribeEffectivePatchesForPatchBaseline on the freshly created baseline) and inspect $.EffectivePatches[0].Patch — it is a '<YYYY-MM> <Classification> for <Product> (KB<n>)' template record with a clock-relative ReleaseDate and an Epoch member, not the recorded KB972455 catalogue entry.
7. Cause 5: replay fixture ssm-catalog-list-documents step task#1 (unfiltered ListDocuments) and compare each DocumentIdentifier's CreatedDate, Categories, DocumentFormat, PlatformTypes, SchemaVersion, TargetType, DocumentType, Author and ReviewStatus against the recording, and check that AWS-ConfigureS3BucketPublicAccessBlock is listed.
